The combination of late nights, irregular meals, and constantly changing schedules culminated in a period where he suddenly felt he could handle everything at once: he began volunteering for every busy shift, pitching multiple new menu concepts to management, and staying up all night sketching business plans for a pop‑up food truck, all while insisting he didn’t need rest. Over the next few months, this overextension at work gave way to a prolonged downturn in which he called out of shifts, stopped playing soccer, and struggled to keep up even basic tasks like paying bills and grocery shopping, putting his job at risk and straining his reputation as a reliable mentor to new staff. When his manager pulled him aside about erratic performance and his mother expressed concern about dramatic changes in his energy and drive, he agreed—reluctantly at first—to see a mental health professional through the clinic his employer’s insurance covered.\n- Chief Complaint: “I go from feeling like I can work nonstop and take on everything to suddenly having no energy to even show up for finished school. \n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): On my high days I just talk and talk—about new menu ideas, my travel plans, random stories from work—jumping from one thing to another so fast that people can’t get a word in and I feel almost physically uncomfortable if there’s a pause in the conversation. \n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My brain can get so revved up that thoughts are flying through faster than I can grab them—one second I’m thinking about paying off my credit card, then I’m planning a trip to Europe, then I’m redesigning the restaurant logo in my head, all at once, and I can’t slow it down enough to focus on anything. \n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): For a while there I was volunteering for every shift, texting old friends to hang out after close, hitting the gym at weird hours, and staying up all night sketching out business plans and menus for this food truck idea, like I had to keep doing things every minute or I’d explode. \n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- can’t get a word in and I feel almost physically uncomfortable if there’s a pause in the conversation. \n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My brain can get so revved up that thoughts are flying through faster I haven't lost my job, but I have been written up for no-shows and mistakes, which is putting my employment at risk, and I've also fallen behind on my rent and bills.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these cycles of high energy and low energy for at least two years, or are these distinct episodes separated by periods where you feel completely like yourself?\n\nPatient: No, this hasn't been going on for two years at all. The whole cycle of highs and lows actually started about four months ago when I began taking on all those extra double shifts at work. The intense high period lasted for a few weeks, but I've been stuck in this heavy, low stretch now for about the last three months.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your symptoms last for at least one full week, or were they shorter in duration?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely lasted longer than a week. That high-energy period went on for about a full month before I crashed. It was a solid few weeks of feeling like I could do absolutely everything without stopping.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you experience any symptoms like hearing voices, seeing things that weren't there, or feeling like you had special powers or abilities?"

I haven't lost my job, but I have been written up for no-shows and mistakes, which is putting my employment at risk, and I've also fallen behind on my rent and bills.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these cycles of high energy and low energy for at least two years, or are these distinct episodes separated by periods where you feel completely like yourself?\n\nPatient: No, this hasn't been going on for two years at all. The whole cycle of highs and lows actually started about four months ago when I began taking on all those extra double shifts at work. The intense high period lasted for a few weeks, but I've been stuck in this heavy, low stretch now for about the last three months.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your symptoms last for at least one full week, or were they shorter in duration?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely lasted longer than a week. That high-energy period went on for about a full month before I crashed. It was a solid few weeks of feeling like I could do absolutely everything without stopping.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you experience any symptoms like hearing voices, seeing things that weren't there, or feeling like you had special powers or abilities?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't hear voices or see things that weren't there, but I did have this overwhelming feeling that I was capable of absolutely anything. I was completely convinced I could easily launch a food truck and run my own restaurant chain in a year, and I even talked to my manager like I knew more than him despite dropping out of college.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your behavior become so disruptive or extreme that it caused a marked impairment in your social or occupational functioning, or did you ever have to be hospitalized to prevent harm to yourself or others?\n\nPatient: I've never been hospitalized, but my behavior definitely caused a lot of disruption in my life. I became so overwhelming that my friends started avoiding me, and I made careless mistakes at work that got me written up and put my job at risk.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your behavior become so extreme that it was clearly noticeable to others as a major change in your personality, or was it something that could still be seen as a 'boost' in your usual productivity?\n\nPatient: It was definitely a major, noticeable change rather than just a productivity boost. My mother got really concerned about my sudden change in energy, and my manager even pulled me aside to talk about my erratic behavior. Plus, my friends found me way too wired and overwhelming to be around during those times."

It was a solid few weeks of feeling like I could do absolutely everything without stopping.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you experience any symptoms like hearing voices, seeing things that weren't there, or feeling like you had special powers or abilities?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't hear voices or see things that weren't there, but I did have this overwhelming feeling that I was capable of absolutely anything. I became so overwhelming that my friends started avoiding me, and I made careless mistakes at work that got me written up and put my job at risk.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your behavior become so extreme that it was clearly noticeable to others as a major change in your personality, or was it something that could still be seen as a 'boost' in your usual productivity?\n\nPatient: It was definitely a major, noticeable change rather than just a productivity boost. My mother got really concerned about my sudden change in energy, and my manager even pulled me aside to talk about my erratic behavior. Plus, my friends found me way too wired and overwhelming to be around during those times.\n\nDoctor: During the three months you've been feeling low, have you experienced symptoms like a complete loss of interest in all activities, significant weight changes, or thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I've completely stopped playing Sunday soccer and cooking new recipes, and now I just sit around scrolling on my phone because everything feels pointless.
